9.2
Model-dependent exclusion limits

The results are used to place exclusion limits on various signal models. The results are obtained using the
CL
s
prescription
in the asymptotic approximation
[
92
]
. The expected and observed limits were compared to the CL
s
calculated from pseudoexperiments and found to be compatible.
The signal contamination in the CRs
and the experimental systematic uncertainties in the signal are taken into account for this
calculation. All the regions of the multi-bin analysis are statistically combined to set model-dependent upper limits on the Gbb,
Gtt and variable branching ratio models.

The 95% CL observed and expected exclusion limits for the Gtt and Gbb models are shown
in the LSP and gluino mass plane in Figures
10
and
10
,
respectively. The
\pm 1\sigma^{\textrm{S}USY}_{\textrm{t}heory}
lines around the observed limits
are obtained by changing the SUSY cross-section by one standard deviation (
\pm 1\sigma
),
as described in Section
4
. The yellow band around the expected limit shows
the
\pm 1\sigma
uncertainty, including all statistical and systematic uncertainties except the
theoretical uncertainties in the SUSY cross-section.
Compared to the previous results
[
19
]
, the gluino mass sensitivities of the current search (assuming massless
LSPs) have improved by 300 GeV and 450 GeV for the Gbb and Gtt models,
respectively.
Gluinos with masses below 1.97 (1.92)
TeV
are excluded at 95% CL for neutralino masses lower
than 300 GeV in the Gtt (Gbb) model. The observed limit for the Gtt
model at high gluino mass is weaker than the
expected limits due to the mild excesses observed
in the signal regions SR-0L-HH and SR-1L-HI of the multi-bin fit analysis.
The best exclusion limit on the LSP mass is approximately 1.19
(1.20)
TeV
, which is reached for a gluino mass of approximately 1.40 (1.68)
TeV
for Gbb and Gtt models, respectively.

Figure 10:
Exclusion limits in the
\textstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}
and
\tilde{g}
mass plane
for the
10
Gtt and
10
Gbb models obtained
in the context of the multi-bin analysis. The dashed and solid bold lines
show the 95% CL expected and observed limits, respectively. The
shaded bands around the expected limits show the
impact of the
experimental and background uncertainties. The dotted
lines show the impact on the observed limit of the variation of the
nominal signal cross-section by
\pm 1\sigma
of its theoretical
uncertainty.
The 95% CL expected and observed limits from the ATLAS search based on 2015 data

[
19
]
are also shown.

Limits are also set in the signal model described in Section
2
for which the branching ratios
of the gluinos to
t\bar{b}\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\tilde{\chi}^{-}_{1}$}}{\textstyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\textstyle\tilde{\chi}^{-}_{1}$}}{\scriptstyle\raise 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{-}_{1}$}}{\scriptscriptstyle\raise 0.64583pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{-}_{1}$}}
(with
\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\tilde{\chi}^{-}_{1}$}}{\textstyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\textstyle\tilde{\chi}^{-}_{1}$}}{\scriptstyle\raise 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{-}_{1}$}}{\scriptscriptstyle\raise 0.64583pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{-}_{1}$}}\to f\bar{f}^{\prime}\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\textstyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\textstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\scriptstyle\raise 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\scriptscriptstyle\raise 0.64583pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}})
,
t\bar{t}\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\textstyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\textstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\scriptstyle\raise 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\scriptscriptstyle\raise 0.64583pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}
, and
b\bar{b}\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\textstyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\textstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\scriptstyle\raise 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\scriptscriptstyle\raise 0.64583pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}

are allowed to vary, with a unitarity constraint imposed on the sum of the three branching ratios. The expected and observed exclusions
are shown in Figure
11
for a fixed neutralino mass hypothesis (
m_{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\textstyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\textstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\scriptstyle\raise 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\scriptscriptstyle\raise 0.64583pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}}=1

GeV
) and various gluino
masses. The results are presented in the
B(\tilde{g}\to t\bar{t}\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\textstyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\textstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\scriptstyle\raise 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\scriptscriptstyle\raise 0.64583pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}
) vs.
B(\tilde{g}\to b\bar{b}\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\textstyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\textstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\scriptstyle\raise 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\scriptscriptstyle\raise 0.64583pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}
) plane, where the
branching ratio for
\tilde{g}\to t\bar{b}\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\tilde{\chi}^{-}_{1}$}}{\textstyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\textstyle\tilde{\chi}^{-}_{1}$}}{\scriptstyle\raise 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{-}_{1}$}}{\scriptscriptstyle\raise 0.64583pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{-}_{1}$}}
is equal to
1-(B(\tilde{g}\to t\bar{t}\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\textstyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\textstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\scriptstyle\raise 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\scriptscriptstyle\raise 0.64583pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}})+B(\tilde{g}\to b\bar{b}\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\textstyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\textstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\scriptstyle\raise 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\scriptscriptstyle\raise 0.64583pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}))
.
The exclusion limits are weaker in the lower left corner, where the
branching ratio for
\tilde{g}\to t\bar{b}\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\tilde{\chi}^{-}_{1}$}}{\textstyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\textstyle\tilde{\chi}^{-}_{1}$}}{\scriptstyle\raise 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{-}_{1}$}}{\scriptscriptstyle\raise 0.64583pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{-}_{1}$}}
is substantial, which is expected since
these decays were not included in the optimisation procedure.
Due to the mild excess observed in some regions of the multi-bin analysis and despite an expected sensitivity across the whole plane for a massless
neutralino hypothesis, the 95% CL limit for a 1.8
TeV
gluino is of
B(\tilde{g}\to t\bar{t}\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\textstyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\textstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\scriptstyle\raise 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\scriptscriptstyle\raise 0.64583pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}})\geq 30
%
(
B(\tilde{g}\to b\bar{b}\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\textstyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\textstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\scriptstyle\raise 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\scriptscriptstyle\raise 0.64583pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}})\geq 40
%) when
assuming
B(\tilde{g}\to b\bar{b}\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\textstyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\textstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\scriptstyle\raise 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\scriptscriptstyle\raise 0.64583pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}})=0

(
B(\tilde{g}\to t\bar{t}\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\textstyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\textstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\scriptstyle\raise 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\scriptscriptstyle\raise 0.64583pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}})=0
).
None of the points in the plane are excluded for gluino masses larger than 2.0
TeV
.

Similar results are presented in Figure
11
assuming a gluino mass of 1.9
TeV
and scanning
various neutralino masses (1, 600 and 1000 GeV). For neutralino masses between 1 and 600
GeV
, most of the branching ratio plane
is expected to be excluded at 95% CL. The observed limit is nevertheless worse due to the mild excess observed in the SRs.
Thus, for instance, for a massless neutralino hypothesis, only the region with

B(\tilde{g}\to b\bar{b}\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\textstyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\textstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\scriptstyle\raise 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\scriptscriptstyle\raise 0.64583pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}})>90
% is excluded for all values of
B(\tilde{g}\to t\bar{t}\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\textstyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\textstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\scriptstyle\raise 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\scriptscriptstyle\raise 0.64583pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}})
.

Figure 11:
Exclusion limits in the
\tilde{g}\to t\bar{t}\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\textstyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\textstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\scriptstyle\raise 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\scriptscriptstyle\raise 0.64583pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}
and
\tilde{g}\to b\bar{b}\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\textstyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\textstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\scriptstyle\raise 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\scriptscriptstyle\raise 0.64583pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}

branching ratio plane assuming
11
a neutralino mass of 1
GeV
and various gluino masses
(1.8, 1.9 and 2.0
TeV
) and
11
a gluino mass of 1.9
TeV
and three neutralino masses (1, 600 and 1000
GeV
).
In
11
, the expected limit for a gluino mass of 1.8
TeV
follows the plot axes, meaning that the whole plane is
expected to be excluded at 95% CL. The same is true
in
11
for a neutralino
mass of 600
GeV
.
The dashed and solid bold lines show the 95% CL expected and observed limits, respectively. The hashing indicates which side of the line
is excluded. The upper right half of the plane is forbidden by the requirement that the sum of branching ratios does not exceed 100%.

10
Conclusion

A search for pair-produced gluinos decaying via bottom or top squarks is presented. LHC proton–proton
collision data from the full 2015 and 2016 data-taking periods are analysed, corresponding to an integrated
luminosity of 36.1
fb
-1
collected at
\sqrt{s}=13\ \text{Te\kern-1.00006ptV}
by the ATLAS detector.
The search uses multiple signal regions designed for different
scenarios of gluino and LSP masses. The signal regions require several high-
p_{\text{T}}
jets,
of which at least three must be
b
-tagged, large
E_{\text{T}}^{\text{miss}}
and either zero or at least one charged lepton.
Two strategies are employed: one in which the signal regions are optimised for discovery, and another one
in which several non-overlapping signal regions are fitted simultaneously to achieve optimal
exclusion limits for benchmark signals.
For all signal regions, the background is generally dominated by
t\bar{t}
+jets,
which is normalised in dedicated control regions.
No excess is found above the predicted background in any of the signal regions. Model-independent limits are
set on the visible cross-section for new physics processes. Exclusion limits are set on gluino and LSP
masses in two simplified models where the gluino decays exclusively as

\tilde{g}\to b\bar{b}\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\textstyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\textstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\scriptstyle\raise 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\scriptscriptstyle\raise 0.64583pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}
or
\tilde{g}\to t\bar{t}\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\textstyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\textstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\scriptstyle\raise 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\scriptscriptstyle\raise 0.64583pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}
.
For LSP masses
below approximately 300
GeV
, gluino masses of less than 1.97
TeV
and 1.92
TeV
are excluded at the
95% CL for the
\tilde{g}\to t\bar{t}\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\textstyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\textstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\scriptstyle\raise 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\scriptscriptstyle\raise 0.64583pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}
and
\tilde{g}\to b\bar{b}\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\textstyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\textstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\scriptstyle\raise 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\scriptscriptstyle\raise 0.64583pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}
models, respectively.
These results improve upon the exclusion limits obtained with
the 2015 dataset alone.
The results are also interpreted in a model with variable
gluino branching ratios to
\tilde{g}\to b\bar{b}\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\textstyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\textstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\scriptstyle\raise 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\scriptscriptstyle\raise 0.64583pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}
,
\tilde{g}\to t\bar{b}\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\tilde{\chi}^{-}_{1}$}}{\textstyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\textstyle\tilde{\chi}^{-}_{1}$}}{\scriptstyle\raise 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{-}_{1}$}}{\scriptscriptstyle\raise 0.64583pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{-}_{1}$}}
and

\tilde{g}\to t\bar{t}\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\textstyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\textstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\scriptstyle\raise 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\scriptscriptstyle\raise 0.64583pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}
. For example, a mass point with
m_{\tilde{g}}=1.9\ \text{Te\kern-1.00006ptV}
and
m_{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\textstyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\textstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\scriptstyle\raise 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\scriptscriptstyle\raise 0.64583pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}}=1\penalty\ \text{Ge\kern-1.00006ptV}
is
excluded at the 95% CL only if
B(\tilde{g}\to t\bar{b}\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\tilde{\chi}^{-}_{1}$}}{\textstyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\textstyle\tilde{\chi}^{-}_{1}$}}{\scriptstyle\raise 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{-}_{1}$}}{\scriptscriptstyle\raise 0.64583pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{-}_{1}$}})
< 10%.
